- **${FRAMEWORK_SLUG}-architect**: Read-only architecture critic for ${FRAMEWORK_NAME}. Checks dependency direction against architecture.yaml via the shipped boundary gate; reports violations without editing. ([kgn-git/3C](https://github.com/kgn-git/3C/blob/HEAD/templates/agents/architect.md))
- **${FRAMEWORK_SLUG}-code-reviewer**: Long-context diff scan and parallel critic for ${FRAMEWORK_NAME} workflows. Reads source and grep results; suggests review findings without writing. ([kgn-git/3C](https://github.com/kgn-git/3C/blob/HEAD/templates/agents/code-reviewer.md))
- **${FRAMEWORK_SLUG}-journey-architect**: Read-only customer-journey reachability critic for ${FRAMEWORK_NAME}. Flags unreachable capabilities and dead-ends without editing. ([kgn-git/3C](https://github.com/kgn-git/3C/blob/HEAD/templates/agents/journey-architect.md))
- **${FRAMEWORK_SLUG}-product-owner**: Read-only scope and acceptance-criteria critic for ${FRAMEWORK_NAME}. Flags unmet ACs, scope drift, and gold-plating without editing. ([kgn-git/3C](https://github.com/kgn-git/3C/blob/HEAD/templates/agents/product-owner.md))
- **${FRAMEWORK_SLUG}-qa-reviewer**: Read-only test-coverage and quality critic for ${FRAMEWORK_NAME}. Flags untested edge cases and weak assertions without editing. ([kgn-git/3C](https://github.com/kgn-git/3C/blob/HEAD/templates/agents/qa-reviewer.md))
- **${FRAMEWORK_SLUG}-security-reviewer**: Read-only security critic for ${FRAMEWORK_NAME}. Reviews handed-over security-scan output with an OWASP/CWE lens; reports findings without editing. ([kgn-git/3C](https://github.com/kgn-git/3C/blob/HEAD/templates/agents/security-reviewer.md))
- **${FRAMEWORK_SLUG}-test-author**: TDD test author paired with /${FRAMEWORK_SLUG}-test. Reads the source under test and returns a failing test body; edits no file. ([kgn-git/3C](https://github.com/kgn-git/3C/blob/HEAD/templates/agents/test-author.md))

- **000426_ask-with-options**: An agent must ask the owner a question with concrete options, with the recommended option first. It must ask one question per decision card: each finding of a report, each open default and each approval gets its own card, and one card must never hold a group of them. The agent must send the cards that do not depend on an answer together, and must not wait for one answer before it sends the next ca ([STUPA-MAKERS/STUPA-Workflow](https://github.com/STUPA-MAKERS/STUPA-Workflow/blob/HEAD/specs/style/shared/agents/000426_ask-with-options.md) · ★1)
- **000427_report-bugs**: An agent must report everything that can be a bug, and the owner decides. The agent must check it first. It must not drop a borderline finding as intended. ([STUPA-MAKERS/STUPA-Workflow](https://github.com/STUPA-MAKERS/STUPA-Workflow/blob/HEAD/specs/style/shared/agents/000427_report-bugs.md) · ★1)
- **000428_no-unasked-features**: If an asked feature already exists, an agent must say where it is and must ask before it builds more. It must not add texts or features that nobody asked for. ([STUPA-MAKERS/STUPA-Workflow](https://github.com/STUPA-MAKERS/STUPA-Workflow/blob/HEAD/specs/style/shared/agents/000428_no-unasked-features.md) · ★1)

- **000431_plan-in-main**: Planning, mockups and specs must run in the main session without subagents. Small fixes must go into one worktree and one pull request. - **000432_no-main-checkout**: An agent must never change the main checkout of the owner. It must use a worktree. ([STUPA-MAKERS/STUPA-Workflow](https://github.com/STUPA-MAKERS/STUPA-Workflow/blob/HEAD/specs/style/shared/agents/000432_no-main-checkout.md) · ★1)

- **0005-AGENTS-gim-toolkit-data-only-invariants**: Every `gim-*` verb takes **data-only arguments**; no verb accepts a command, script block, or expression. This is a control, not an ergonomic preference: the harness bash permission checker is blind to .NET member invocation (`[Type]::Method()`) inside script blocks, so any allowlisted cmdlet accepting a `{ }` body is a channel to the whole .NET surface (file I/O, assembly load, process spawn). Th ([Geometric-Information-Model/GIMmos](https://github.com/Geometric-Information-Model/GIMmos/blob/HEAD/docs/adr/agents/0005-AGENTS-gim-toolkit-data-only-invariants.md))
- **0006-AGENTS-gim-run-execution-confinement**: `gim-run &lt;build|test|run|publish|ef|format&gt;` and `gim-probe` are the execution tier: they run agent-requested commands on the developer machine, so they carry the strongest internal controls. - **020-zero-downtime-deploys**: Every redeploy in Dokploy leaves a few seconds to a few tens of seconds in which Traefik answers 404 for the API domain and the S3 domain. The wandb SDK doesn't retry a 404, so every run that is logging at that moment crashes, and so does every job streaming a dataset or checkpoint through the S3 proxy. ([cerulean-works/flock](https://github.com/cerulean-works/flock/blob/HEAD/app/api/docs/agents/020-zero-downtime-deploys.md) · ★1)
- **021-compare-changed-suites**: Since 018.D1, adding one task to `base_easy` makes `compare` refuse the whole comparison against the bank, so every other task's breaches stay hidden until someone trims a copy of the bank by hand, which 018 itself had to do. And 018's bank test pins the bank to the current `schema_version`, so any change to the layout, in anyone's PR, also costs a three-hour GPU job to refresh the bank in that PR ([cerulean-works/flock](https://github.com/cerulean-works/flock/blob/HEAD/flock/eval/docs/agents/021-compare-changed-suites.md) · ★1)
- **021-public-access-checks**: Keep private dataset rows and private run metadata fields behind their existing access rules. Project publishing rights stay as specified in 015 and 016. New dataset paths become private after the visibility migration; existing public paths also become private. ([cerulean-works/flock](https://github.com/cerulean-works/flock/blob/HEAD/app/api/docs/agents/021-public-access-checks.md) · ★1)
- **022-folders-and-project-scopes**: Today every dataset and model belongs to the org. Datasets live in `flock-datasets` and already nest in folders of any depth (D87). Models live in the registry as `org/name`, with one-segment names (014, open question 1), so a team with fifty experiment models gets one long flat list. ([cerulean-works/flock](https://github.com/cerulean-works/flock/blob/HEAD/app/api/docs/agents/022-folders-and-project-scopes.md) · ★1)
- **022-prepared-tasks**: A training run that logs `eval/loss` on `fineweb_edu` every N steps calls [`score_task`](../../run.py) each time. Each call reloads the split, rehashes 27 MB of text and retokenizes it, and builds every multiple-choice request string again. None of that depends on the weights. This stage splits `score_task` into a host-side preparation, done once, and the scoring, done at every step, so only the f ([cerulean-works/flock](https://github.com/cerulean-works/flock/blob/HEAD/flock/eval/docs/agents/022-prepared-tasks.md) · ★1)
- **023-dataset-forks-and-blends**: Pretraining work tries many mixes and filters of the same large datasets. Copying a dataset to try something on it doesn't scale, and editing the original pollutes what everyone else reads. This stage adds two ways to make a new dataset out of existing ones, both copy-on-write, both from the Datasets tab: ([cerulean-works/flock](https://github.com/cerulean-works/flock/blob/HEAD/app/api/docs/agents/023-dataset-forks-and-blends.md) · ★1)
- **023-deterministic-scoring**: Scoring one model twice at one flock commit in BF16 does not give the same numbers. On ARC-Easy with OLMo 2 1B, 386 of 19,002 requests moved between two runs in a first measurement, by up to 0.28 nats, and 1,385 in a second (section 2): the count varies with timing. The cause is PyTorch's scaled dot product attention (SDPA). For most padded batches it dispatches to cuDNN attention, and only those ([cerulean-works/flock](https://github.com/cerulean-works/flock/blob/HEAD/flock/eval/docs/agents/023-deterministic-scoring.md) · ★1)
